since (contrary to the
theorem statement) it is not typable under a non-null typing environment.

Case
TCG-Free

Assumption:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi}{\{E\}\;\textbf{{free}}\mapsto M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi;\,\mathbb{1}}

By the IH (statement 2), we have that

\Xi(\Theta)\vdash M\,{:}\,\Xi(\tau)
.

Trivially,
E\vDash_{\textsf{lit}}\mathbb{1}
.

Therefore, we can reconstruct by
TG-Free
:

\frac{\Xi(\Theta)\vdash M\,{:}\,\Xi(\tau)}{\Xi(\Theta)\vdash\textbf{{free}}\mapsto M\,{:}\,\Xi(\tau)\;{::}\;\mathbb{1}}

as required.

Case
TCG-Recv

Assumption:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\sigma\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime},y:\texttt{?}\delta^{{\bullet}};\,\Phi_{1}\quad\mathcal{P}({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})={\overrightarrow{\pi}}\quad\Theta=\Theta^{\prime}\mathop{-}\overrightarrow{x}\quad\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{\pi})\vee\textsf{base}(\Theta^{\prime})\quad\textsf{check}(\Theta^{\prime},\overrightarrow{x},\overrightarrow{\lceil\pi\rceil})=\Phi_{3}}{\{E\}\;\textbf{{receive}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\texttt{[}\overrightarrow{x}\texttt{]}\;\textbf{{from}}\;y\mapsto M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,\sigma\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}\cup\Phi_{3}\cup\{E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta\};\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})}\quad\textsc{TCG-Recv}

We also assume that we have some usable solution
\Xi

for

\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}\cup\Phi_{3}\cup\{E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta\}
.

As usual, by Lemma
D.26
we can assume that
\Xi

is a usable solution for all
\Phi_{i}
.

By the IH,

\Xi(\Theta^{\prime}),y:\texttt{?}\Xi(\delta)^{{\bullet}}\vdash M\,{:}\,\Xi(\sigma)
.

Suppose

\Theta^{\prime}=\Theta,x_{1}:\tau_{1},\ldots,x_{m}:\tau_{m}
and

\overrightarrow{x}=x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}
.

Then by the definition of
check
we have that:

•

(\tau_{i}\leq\lceil\pi_{i}\rceil\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in 1..m}

•

(\textsf{unr}(\tau_{i})\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in m+1..n}

Thus by Lemma
D.28
,

\lceil\Xi(\pi_{i})\rceil\leq\Xi(\tau_{i})
for each
i\in 1..m
.

By Lemma
D.30
, there exist
A_{j}\leq\Xi(\tau_{j})
such that

\textsf{un}(A_{j})
for
j\in{m+1}..n
.

Thus it follows by the definition of environment subtyping that

\Xi(\Theta)\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
.

It follows from the fact that pattern substitution preserves type shape
that if
\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{T})\vee\textsf{base}(\Theta^{\prime})
, we have that

\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{\Xi(T)})\vee\textsf{base}(\Xi(\Theta^{\prime}))
.

Since
\Xi
is a usable solution of

E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta
we know that

E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\sqsubseteq\Xi(\delta)
and therefore that

\texttt{?}(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})\leq\texttt{?}(\Xi(\delta))
.

It remains to be shown that
E\vDash_{\textsf{lit}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})
:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})\simeq E}{E\vDash_{\textsf{lit}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})}

The pattern residual and concatenation cancel, so the premise holds and
therefore we can conclude that

E\vDash_{\textsf{lit}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})
.

Finally, we can reconstruct using
TG-Recv
:

\frac{\Xi(\mathcal{P})({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})=\overrightarrow{\Xi(\pi)}\quad\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{\Xi(\pi)})\vee\textsf{base}(\Xi(\Theta))\quad\frac{\Xi(\Theta^{\prime}),y:\texttt{?}\Xi(\delta)^{{\bullet}}\vdash M\,{:}\,\Xi(\sigma)}{\Xi(\Theta),y:\texttt{?}(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})^{{\bullet}},\overrightarrow{x}:\overrightarrow{\lceil T\rceil}\vdash M\,{:}\,\Xi(\sigma)}}{\Xi(\Theta)\vdash\textbf{{receive}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\texttt{[}\overrightarrow{x}\texttt{]}\;\textbf{{from}}\;y\mapsto M\,{:}\,\Xi(\sigma)\;{::}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})}

as required.

∎

See
4.4

Proof.

A direct consequence of Lemma
D.39
.
∎

D.4.
Algorithmic Completeness

Every
\Gamma
is also a valid
\Theta
and every
A
is a valid

\tau
. We will therefore allow ourselves to use
\Gamma
and
A
in
algorithmic type system derivations directly.

Definition D.40 (Closed program).

A program
(\mathcal{S},\overrightarrow{D},M)
is
closed

if
\textsf{pv}(\mathcal{S})=\emptyset
and
\textsf{pv}(\overrightarrow{D})=\emptyset
.

D.4.1.
Useful auxiliary lemmas

We begin by stating two useful results. The first lemma states that values are
typable in the algorithmic system without constraints.

Lemma D.41.

If
\Gamma\vdash V\,{:}\,A
, then there exists some

\Gamma^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
and

V\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Gamma^{\prime};\,\emptyset
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash V\,{:}\,A
.

Case
T-Var

We assume that
x:A\vdash x\,{:}\,A
.
By
TC-Var
we can show
x\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,x:A;\,\emptyset
,
as required.

Case
T-Const

We assume that
\cdot\vdash c\,{:}\,C
, where
c
has base type

C
. By
TS-Base
, we can show that

c\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;C\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\cdot;\,\emptyset
.
Finally, by
TC-Sub
(noting that

C\leq C\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\emptyset
) we have that

c\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;C\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\cdot;\,\emptyset
, as required.

Case
T-Sub

Assumption:

\frac{\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}\quad A\leq B\quad\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash V\,{:}\,A}{\Gamma\vdash V\,{:}\,B}

By the IH, there exists some
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

and

\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;V\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,A;\,\emptyset
.

By the transitivity of subtyping, we have that

\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, as required.

∎

Lemma D.42.

If
M\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi
, then

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi
.

Proof.

Follows from the definition of
TC-Subs
, noting that the subtyping
constraint is instantiated as
\tau\leq\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi
.
There are two ways we can create a derivation of

\tau\leq\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi
: either if
\tau=C
and we have

\tau\leq\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\emptyset
, or if
\tau
is a mailbox type (take an
output mailbox type here, although the reasoning is the same for an input
mailbox). In this case, we would have a derivation of

\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta}\leq\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\gamma\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma
.
Since
\gamma\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma
is a tautology, it follows that we
need not add an additional constraint and can show

\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta}\leq\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\emptyset
.

Using
TC-Subs
we can construct:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi\quad\tau\leq\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\emptyset}{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi}

as required.
∎

D.4.2.
Completeness of auxiliary definitions

We now need to show completeness for all auxiliary judgements (e.g., subtyping,
environment combination).

Lemma D.43 (Completeness of type join).

If:

•

A_{1}\triangleright A_{2}=B
,

•

A_{1}\leq\Xi_{1}(\tau_{1})
,

•

A_{2}\leq\Xi_{2}(\tau_{2})
; and

•

\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset

then there exist
\Theta,\Phi
such that

\tau_{1}\fatsemi\tau_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\sigma;\Phi
,
and there exists a usable solution
\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi

such that
B\leq\Xi(\sigma)
.

Proof.

We proceed by case analysis on the derivation of

A_{1}\triangleright A_{2}=B
.
Base types follow straightforwardly, so we concentrate on mailbox types.

Case
A_{1}=\texttt{!}E_{1}^{\eta_{1}}
,
A_{2}=\texttt{!}E_{2}^{\eta_{2}}

Assumption:

\frac{\frac{}{\texttt{!}E_{1}\triangleright\texttt{!}E_{2}=\texttt{!}E_{1}\odot E_{2}}}{\texttt{!}E_{1}^{\eta_{1}}\triangleright\texttt{!}E_{2}^{\eta_{2}}=\texttt{!}(E_{1}\odot E_{2})^{\eta_{1}\triangleright\eta_{2}}}

Since the domains of
\Xi_{1},\Xi_{2}
are disjoint, let

\Xi=\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
.

In order for
\texttt{!}E_{i}\leq\Xi_{i}(\tau_{i})
(for
i\in 1,2
) to hold, it must be the case that
\tau_{i}=\texttt{!}\gamma_{i}

with
\Xi(\gamma_{i})\sqsubseteq E_{i}
.

In this case we can construct a derivation:

\frac{\texttt{!}\gamma_{1}\fatsemi\texttt{!}\gamma_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{!}(\gamma_{1}\odot\gamma_{2});\emptyset}{\texttt{!}\gamma_{1}^{\eta_{1}}\fatsemi\texttt{!}\gamma_{2}^{\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{!}(\gamma_{1}\odot\gamma_{2})^{\eta_{1}\triangleright\eta_{2}};\emptyset}

Noting that
\Xi(\gamma_{i})\sqsubseteq E_{i}
,
it follows by the definition of pattern semantics that

\Xi(\gamma_{1}\odot\gamma_{2})\sqsubseteq E_{1}\odot E_{2}

and therefore that

\texttt{!}(\gamma_{1}\odot\gamma_{2})^{\eta_{1}\triangleright\eta_{2}}\leq\Xi(\texttt{!}(\gamma_{1}\odot\gamma_{2}))^{\eta_{1}\triangleright\eta_{2}}

with
\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
and a solution
of
\emptyset
, as required.

Case
A_{1}=\texttt{!}E^{\eta_{1}},A_{2}=\texttt{?}(E\odot F)^{\eta_{2}}

Assumption:

\frac{\frac{}{\texttt{!}E\triangleright\texttt{?}(E\odot F)=\texttt{?}F}}{\texttt{!}E^{\eta_{1}}\triangleright\texttt{?}(E\odot F)^{\eta_{2}}=F^{\eta_{1}\triangleright\eta_{2}}}

Since the domains of
\Xi_{1},\Xi_{2}
are disjoint, let

\Xi=\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
.

In order for

\texttt{!}E\leq\Xi_{1}(\tau_{1})

to hold, it must be the case that
\tau_{1}=\texttt{!}\gamma

with
\Xi_{1}(\gamma)\sqsubseteq E
.

Similarly, for

\texttt{?}(E\odot F)\leq\Xi_{2}(\tau_{2})

to hold, it must be the case that
\tau_{2}=\texttt{?}\delta

with
(E\odot F)\sqsubseteq\Xi_{2}(\delta)
.

In this case we can construct a derivation:
Using algorithmic type joining, we can construct the following
derivation:

\frac{\frac{\alpha\text{ fresh}}{\texttt{!}\gamma\fatsemi\texttt{?}\delta\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{?}\alpha;\{\delta\odot\alpha\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta\}}}{\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta_{1}}\fatsemi\texttt{?}\delta^{\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{?}\alpha^{\eta_{1}\triangleright\eta_{2}};\{\gamma\odot\alpha\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta\}}

At this point we know that the domains of
\Xi_{1}
and

\Xi_{2}
are disjoint. Let us construct
\Xi=\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}\cup\alpha\mapsto F
.

It remains to be shown that
\Xi
is a solution; it suffices to
show that
(\Xi(\gamma)\odot F)\sqsubseteq\Xi(\delta)
.

By the transitivity of pattern inclusion we have that

(\Xi(\gamma)\odot F)\sqsubseteq(E\odot F)\sqsubseteq\Xi(\delta)

We have that
\Xi(\texttt{?}\alpha)=\texttt{?}F

and therefore we have that (trivially)

\texttt{?}F\leq\texttt{?}F

with
\Xi\supset\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
a solution for the
constraint set, as required.

Case
A_{1}=\texttt{?}E\odot F^{\eta_{1}},A_{2}=\texttt{!}E^{\eta_{2}}

Similar to the above case.

∎

Lemma D.44 (Completeness of environment join).

If:

•

\Gamma_{1}\triangleright\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma
,

•

\Gamma_{1}\preccurlyeq\Xi_{1}(\Theta_{1})
,

•

\Gamma_{2}\preccurlyeq\Xi_{2}(\Theta_{2})
; and

•

\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset

then there exist
\Theta,\Phi
such that

\Theta_{1}\fatsemi\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi
,
and there exists a usable solution
\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi

such that
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma_{1}\triangleright\Gamma_{2}
, with appeal to
Lemma
D.43
.
∎

Lemma D.45 (Completeness of disjoint environment combination).

If:

•

\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma
,

•

\Gamma_{1}\preccurlyeq\Xi_{1}(\Theta_{1})
,

•

\Gamma_{2}\preccurlyeq\Xi_{2}(\Theta_{2})
; and

•

\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset

then there exist
\Theta,\Phi
such that

\Theta_{1}+\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi
,
and there exists a usable solution
\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi

such that
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma
.

Case

\Gamma_{1}=\cdot
and
\Gamma_{2}=\cdot

\frac{}{\cdot+\cdot=\cdot}

By the definition of environment subtyping, the only environment that can
be a supertype of the empty environment is
\cdot
. Therefore,
we can immediately conclude with the corresponding base case in
algorithmic type environment combination:

\frac{}{\cdot+\cdot\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\cdot;\emptyset}

Case

x\not\in\textsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})

Assumption:

\frac{x\not\in\textsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})\quad\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma}{\Gamma_{1},x:A+\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma,x:A}

where:

•

\Gamma_{1},x:A\preccurlyeq\Xi_{1}(\Theta_{1})

•

\Gamma_{2}\preccurlyeq\Xi_{2}(\Theta_{2})

•

\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset

Since we are considering strict subtyping on environments rather than
general subtyping, we can assume that
x:A\in\textsf{dom}(\Theta_{1})
.
Therefore, let
\Theta_{1}=\Theta^{\prime}_{1},x:\tau
with
A\leq\Xi_{1}(\tau)
.

By the IH,

\Theta^{\prime}_{1}+\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi

for some
\Theta,\Phi
and
there exists some usable solution

\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi

such that
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\leq\Xi(\Theta)
.

Since
A\leq\Xi_{1}(\tau)
and
\Xi_{1}\subseteq\Xi
, it follows that
A\leq\Xi(\tau)
.

Therefore it follows that
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2},x:A\leq\Xi(\Theta,x:\tau)
as required.

Case

x\not\in\textsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})

\frac{x\not\in\textsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\quad\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma}{\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2},x:A=\Gamma,x:A}

Symmetric to the above case.

Case

x\in\textsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\cap\textsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})

\frac{\textsf{un}(A)\quad\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma}{\Gamma_{1},x:A+\Gamma_{2},x:A=\Gamma,x:A}

In this case, we have that:

•

\Theta_{1},=\Theta^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma_{1}

•

\Theta_{2}=\Theta^{\prime}_{2},x:\sigma_{2}

By the IH, there exist
\Theta,\Phi
such that

\Theta^{\prime}_{1}+\Theta^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi

and some usable solution
\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi

such that
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
.

By algorithmic environment combination we have:

\frac{\Theta^{\prime}_{1}+\Theta^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi_{1}\quad{\sigma_{1}\sim\sigma_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi_{2}}\quad{\textsf{unr}(\sigma_{1})\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi_{3}}\quad{\textsf{unr}(\sigma_{2})\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi_{4}}}{\Theta^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma_{1}+\Theta^{\prime}_{2},x:\sigma_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta,x:B_{1};\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}\cup\Phi_{3}\cup\Phi_{4}}

From
\textsf{un}(A)
, we have two subcases based on whether
A
is a base type

C
, or a mailbox type
\texttt{!}\mathbb{1}^{\circ}
.

Subcase
A=C

In this case, by the definition of subtyping we have that

B_{1}=B_{2}=C
and therefore:

\frac{\Theta^{\prime}_{1}+\Theta^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi\quad{C\sim C\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\emptyset}\quad{\textsf{unr}(C)\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\emptyset}\quad{\textsf{unr}(C)\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\emptyset}}{\Theta^{\prime}_{1},x:C+\Theta^{\prime}_{2},x:C\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta,x:C;\Phi}

with
\Xi
remaining a usable solution of
\Phi
.

It follows that
\Theta^{\prime}_{1},x:C+\Theta^{\prime}_{2},x:C\leq\Xi(\Theta),x:C
, as required.

Subcase
A=\texttt{!}\mathbb{1}^{\circ}

In this case, we have that

B_{1}=\texttt{!}\delta_{1}^{\circ}
and

B_{2}=\texttt{!}\delta_{2}^{\circ}
.

and:

\frac{\Theta^{\prime}_{1}+\Theta^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi\quad{\texttt{!}\delta_{1}^{\circ}\sim\texttt{!}\delta_{2}^{\circ}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\{\delta_{1}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta_{2},\delta_{2}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta_{1}\}}\quad{\textsf{unr}(\delta_{1})\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\{\delta_{1}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\mathbb{1}\}}\quad{\textsf{unr}(\delta_{2})\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\{\delta_{2}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\mathbb{1}\}}}{\Theta^{\prime}_{1},\texttt{!}\delta_{1}^{\circ}+\Theta^{\prime}_{2},\texttt{!}\delta_{2}^{\circ}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta,x:\texttt{!}\delta_{1}^{\circ};\Phi\cup\{\delta_{1}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta_{2},\delta_{2}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta_{1},\delta_{1}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\mathbb{1},\delta_{2}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\mathbb{1}\}}

Let
\Xi^{\prime}=\Xi[\delta_{1}\mapsto\mathbb{1},\delta_{2}\mapsto\mathbb{1}]
, which is now a usable solution for the
additional constraints.

Finally, we have that
\Gamma,x:\texttt{!}\mathbb{1}^{\circ}\leq\Xi^{\prime}(\Theta,x:\texttt{!}\delta_{1}^{\circ})\leq\Xi^{\prime}(\Theta),x:\texttt{!}\mathbb{1}^{\circ}
, as required.

∎

As a corollary we can show the completeness of combining nullable environments:

Corollary D.46.

If:

•

\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma
,

•

\Gamma_{1}\leq\Xi_{1}(\Psi_{1})
,

•

\Gamma_{2}\leq\Xi_{2}(\Psi_{2})
; and

•

\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset

then there exist
\Theta,\Phi
such that

\Psi_{1}+\Psi_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi
,
and there exists a usable solution
\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi

such that
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
.

Next, we need to show completeness of merging.

Lemma D.47.

If:

•

A\leq\Xi_{1}(\tau_{1})
,

•

A\leq\Xi_{2}(\tau_{2})
; and

•

\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset

then there exist
\tau,\Phi
such that

\tau_{1}\sqcap\tau_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\sigma;\Phi

and there exists a usable solution

\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi

such that
A\leq\Xi(\sigma)
.

Proof.

By case analysis on the structure of
A
.

Base types follow directly, so we need instead to examine mailbox types.

Case
A=\texttt{!}E^{\eta}

In this case, by the definition of subtyping, we have that:

•

\texttt{!}E^{\eta}\leq\Xi_{1}(\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta_{1}})

•

\texttt{!}E^{\eta}\leq\Xi_{2}(\texttt{!}\delta^{\eta_{2}})

We first show that
\eta\leq\textsf{max}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})
.
If
\eta=\circ
then it must be the case that
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}=\circ
and
\textsf{max}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})=\circ
.
If
\eta={\bullet}
then we have that
\eta\leq\textsf{max}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})
.

Using algorithmic type merging, we can construct:

\frac{\texttt{!}\gamma\sqcap\texttt{!}\delta\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{!}(\gamma\oplus\delta);\emptyset}{\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta_{1}}\sqcap\texttt{!}\delta^{\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{!}(\gamma\oplus\delta)^{\textsf{max}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})};\emptyset}

Since
\textsf{dom}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{dom}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset
, we can set

\Xi=\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
(which is trivially a
solution of
\emptyset
).

It remains to be shown that
\texttt{!}E^{\eta}\leq\texttt{!}(\Xi(\gamma\oplus\delta))^{\textsf{max}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})}
.

First we note that:

\Xi(\texttt{!}(\gamma\oplus\delta))=\texttt{!}(\Xi(\gamma)\oplus\Xi(\delta))

Now since
\texttt{!}E\leq\texttt{!}\Xi(\gamma)
and

\texttt{!}E\leq\texttt{!}\Xi(\delta)
, it follows by the
definition of subtyping that
\Xi(\gamma)\sqsubseteq E
and

\Xi(\delta)\sqsubseteq E
. Therefore it follows by the definition
of pattern semantics that
\Xi(\gamma)\oplus\Xi(\delta)\sqsubseteq E
and therefore that
\texttt{!}E^{\eta}\leq\texttt{!}(\Xi(\gamma\oplus\delta))^{\textsf{max}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})}
as required.

Case
A=\texttt{?}E^{\eta}

In this case, by the definition of subtyping, we have that:

•

\texttt{!}E^{\eta}\leq\Xi_{1}(\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta_{1}})

•

\texttt{!}E^{\eta}\leq\Xi_{2}(\texttt{!}\delta^{\eta_{2}})

The reasoning for usage subtyping follows from the previous case, so we
take for given that
\eta\leq\textsf{max}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})
.

Next, we construct the following derivation using algorithmic type
merging:

\frac{\frac{\alpha\text{ fresh}}{\texttt{?}\gamma\sqcap\texttt{?}\delta\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{?}\alpha;\{\alpha\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma,\alpha\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta\}}}{\eta_{1}^{\eta}{\texttt{?}\gamma}\sqcap\eta_{2}^{\eta}{\texttt{?}\delta}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\textsf{max}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})^{\eta}{\texttt{?}\alpha};\{\alpha\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma,\alpha\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta\}}

Since
\textsf{dom}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{dom}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset
, we can set

\Xi=\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}\cup\{\alpha\mapsto E\}
.

To show that
\Xi
is a usable solution of the constraint set, it remains to be shown that:

•

E\sqsubseteq\Xi(\gamma)

•

E\sqsubseteq\Xi(\delta)

Since
\texttt{?}E\leq\texttt{?}\Xi_{1}(\gamma)
it
follows that
E\sqsubseteq\Xi_{1}(\gamma)
and likewise
for
\Xi_{2}(\delta)
; since
\textsf{dom}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{dom}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset
it follows that

\texttt{?}E\sqsubseteq\Xi(\gamma)
and likewise for
\delta
, as
required.

∎

Lemma D.48.

If:

•

\Gamma\preccurlyeq\Xi_{1}(\Theta_{1})
,

•

\Gamma\preccurlyeq\Xi_{2}(\Theta_{2})
; and

•

\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset

then there exist
\Theta,\Phi
such that

\Theta_{1}\sqcap\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi

and there exists a usable solution

\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi

such that
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
.

Proof.

By induction on the size of
\Gamma
and inspection of
\Theta_{1}
and

\Theta_{2}
, noting that due to the definition of
\preccurlyeq
, all must be
of the same length; merging of types relies on Lemma
D.47
.
∎

Corollary D.49 (Completeness of merging (nullable environments)).

If:

•

\Gamma\preccurlyeq\Xi_{1}(\Psi_{1})
,

•

\Gamma\preccurlyeq\Xi_{2}(\Psi_{2})
; and

•

\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{1})\cap\textsf{pv}(\Xi_{2})=\emptyset

then there exist
\Theta,\Phi
such that

\Theta_{1}\sqcap\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi;\Phi

and there exists a usable solution

\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi

such that
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Psi)
.

Lemma D.50 (Completeness of subtyping).

If
\Xi(\tau)\leq\Xi(\sigma)
then
\tau\leq\sigma\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi

where
\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi
.

Proof.

By case analysis on
\Xi(\tau)\leq\Xi(\tau)
.

Case
C\leq C

Here we can show
C\leq C\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\emptyset
,
where
\Xi
is trivially a usable solution of
\emptyset
, as required.

Case

\Xi(\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta_{1}})\leq\Xi(\texttt{!}\delta^{\eta_{2}})

Since
\Xi(\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta_{2}})=\texttt{!}\Xi(\gamma)^{\eta_{2}}
we can assume:

\frac{\eta_{1}\leq\eta_{2}\quad\Xi(\delta)\sqsubseteq\Xi(\gamma)}{{\texttt{!}\Xi(\gamma)^{\eta_{1}}}\leq{\texttt{!}\Xi(\delta)^{\eta_{2}}}}

Using algorithmic subtyping we can derive:

\frac{\eta_{1}\leq\eta_{2}\quad\frac{}{\texttt{!}\gamma\leq\texttt{!}\delta\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\delta\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma}}{\texttt{!}\gamma^{\eta_{1}}\leq\texttt{!}\delta^{\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\delta\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma}

And since
\Xi(\gamma)\sqsubseteq E
it follows that
\Xi

is a usable solution of
\delta\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma
, as required.

Case

\texttt{?}\gamma^{\eta_{1}}\leq\Xi(\texttt{?}\delta^{\eta_{2}})

Since
\Xi(\texttt{?}\delta^{\eta_{2}})=\texttt{?}\Xi(\delta)^{\eta_{2}}
we can assume:

\frac{\eta_{1}\leq\eta_{2}\quad\Xi(\gamma)\sqsubseteq\Xi(\gamma)}{{\texttt{?}\Xi(\gamma)^{\eta_{1}}}\leq{\texttt{?}\Xi(\delta)^{\eta_{2}}}}

Using algorithmic subtyping we can derive:

\frac{\eta_{1}\leq\eta_{2}\quad\frac{}{\texttt{?}\gamma\leq\texttt{?}\delta\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\gamma\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta}}{\texttt{?}\gamma^{\eta_{1}}\leq\texttt{?}\delta^{\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\gamma\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta}

And since
\Xi(\gamma)\sqsubseteq\Xi(\delta)
it follows that
\Xi

is a usable solution of
\gamma\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta
, as required.

∎

Lemma D.51 (Completeness of check meta-function).

If
\Xi(\Theta,x:\tau)\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
then

\textsf{check}(\Theta^{\prime},x,\tau)=\Phi
where
\Xi
is a usable
solution of
\Phi
.

Proof.

A direct consequence of Lemma
D.50
.
∎

The
n
-ary version of Lemma
D.51
follows as a corollary:

Corollary D.52 (Completeness of n-ary check meta-function).

If
\Xi(\Theta,\overrightarrow{x}:\overrightarrow{\tau})\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
then

\textsf{check}(\Theta^{\prime},\overrightarrow{x},\overrightarrow{\tau})=\Phi
where
\Xi
is a usable
solution of
\Phi
.

D.4.3.
Supertype checkability

In order to show the completeness of
T-Sub
, we must show that if a
term is checkable at a subtype, then it is also checkable at a supertype.

To do this we require several intermediate results.

We firstly define
closed
and
satisfiable
constraint sets.

Definition D.53 (Closed constraint set).

A constraint set
\Phi
is
closed
if
\textsf{pv}(\Phi)=\emptyset
.

Definition D.54 (Satisfiable constraint set).

A closed constraint set
\Phi
is
satisfiable
if the empty
solution is a solution for
\Phi

(i.e.,
\Phi=(E_{i}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}F_{i})_{i}

and
(E_{i}\sqsubseteq F_{i})_{i}
).

If we have two types which do not contain pattern variables, algorithmic
subtyping does not introduce any pattern variables into the constraint set.

Lemma D.55 (Subtyping introduces no fresh variables).

If
A\leq B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi
, then
\textsf{pv}(\Phi)=\emptyset
.

Proof.

A straightforward case analysis on the derivation of

\tau\leq\sigma\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi
.
∎

Next, if we have an algorithmic subtyping judgement which produces a satisfiable
constraint set, and a subtyping relation with a supertype, then we can show
that the algorithmic subtyping judgement instantiated with the supertype will
produce a satisfiable constraint set.

Lemma D.56 (Widening of algorithmic subtyping).

If
A\leq A^{\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi
where
\Phi
is satisfiable,
and
A^{\prime}\leq B
, then

A\leq B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi^{\prime}

and
\Phi^{\prime}
is satisfiable.

Proof.

By case analysis on the derivation of
A\leq A^{\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi
.

Base types hold trivially, so we need only consider two cases:

Case
\texttt{!}E\leq\texttt{!}F

Assumption:

\frac{\eta_{1}\leq\eta_{2}}{\texttt{!}E^{\eta_{1}}\leq\texttt{!}F^{\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\{F\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}E\}}

also we know that
F\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}E
is satisfiable (therefore
that
F\sqsubseteq E
), and

\texttt{!}F\leq B
.

By the definition of subtyping we have that
B=\texttt{!}F^{\prime}
for
some pattern
F^{\prime}
, and therefore that
F^{\prime}\sqsubseteq F
.

By transitivity we have that

F^{\prime}\sqsubseteq F\sqsubseteq E
and therefore

\frac{\eta_{1}\leq\eta_{2}}{\texttt{!}E^{\eta_{1}}\leq\texttt{!}F^{\prime\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\{F^{\prime}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}E\}}

where
F^{\prime}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}E
is satisfiable, as required.

Case
\texttt{?}E\leq\texttt{?}F

Assumption:

\frac{\eta_{1}\leq\eta_{2}}{\texttt{?}E^{\eta_{1}}\leq\texttt{?}F^{\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\{E\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}F\}}

also we know that
E\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}F
is satisfiable (therefore
that
E\sqsubseteq F
), and

\texttt{?}F\leq B
.

By the definition of subtyping we have that
B=\texttt{?}F^{\prime}
for
some pattern
F^{\prime}
and
therefore that
F\sqsubseteq F^{\prime}
.

Thus by transitivity we have that
E\sqsubseteq F\sqsubseteq F^{\prime}
and therefore that:

\frac{\eta_{1}\leq\eta_{2}}{\texttt{?}E^{\eta_{1}}\leq\texttt{?}F^{\prime\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\{E\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}F^{\prime}\}}

where
E\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}F^{\prime}
is satisfiable, as required.

∎

We also need to show that environment joining respects subtyping, which we do by
firstly showing that type joining respects subtyping.

Lemma D.57 (Algorithmic type join respects subtyping).

If:

•

\tau_{1}\fatsemi\tau_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi

•

\Xi
is some usable solution of
\Phi
such that

\Xi(\tau_{2})\leq\Xi(\tau_{3})
for some
\tau_{3}

then

\tau_{1}\fatsemi\tau_{3}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\tau^{\prime};\Phi^{\prime}
for some
\tau^{\prime}
,

\Phi^{\prime}
such that
\Xi(\tau)\leq\Xi(\tau^{\prime})
and
\Xi
is
a usable solution of
\Xi(\Phi^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Base type combination follows straightforwardly, so we have:

\frac{\varsigma_{1}\fatsemi\varsigma_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\varsigma;\Phi}{\varsigma_{1}^{\eta_{1}}\fatsemi\varsigma_{2}^{\eta_{2}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\varsigma^{{\eta_{1}}\triangleright{\eta_{2}}}{\Phi};}

so it suffices to proceed by case analysis on the derivation of

\varsigma_{1}\fatsemi\varsigma_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\varsigma;\Phi
.

Case
\varsigma_{1}=\texttt{!}\gamma
and
\varsigma_{2}=\texttt{!}\delta

Assumption:

\frac{}{\texttt{!}\gamma\fatsemi\texttt{!}\delta\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{!}(\gamma\odot\delta);\emptyset}

We also assume that
\Xi(\texttt{!}\delta)\leq\Xi(\tau)
for
some
\tau
, which by the definition of subtyping means that
\tau=\texttt{!}\delta^{\prime}
for some
\delta^{\prime}
, where

\Xi(\delta^{\prime})\sqsubseteq\Xi(\delta)
.

It follows by the compositionality of pattern semantics that

\gamma\odot\delta^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\gamma\odot\delta
and thus

\texttt{!}(\gamma\odot\delta)\leq\texttt{!}(\gamma\odot\delta^{\prime})
, and we have that

\frac{}{\texttt{!}\gamma\fatsemi\texttt{!}\delta^{\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{!}(\gamma\odot\delta^{\prime});\emptyset}

as required.

Case
\varsigma_{1}=\texttt{!}\gamma
and
\varsigma_{2}=\texttt{?}\delta

Assumption:

\frac{\alpha{\text{ fresh}}}{\texttt{!}\gamma\fatsemi\texttt{?}\delta\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{?}\alpha;\{(\gamma\odot\alpha)\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta\}}

By the assumptions we know that
\Xi
is a usable solution of

\Phi
such that
\Xi(\texttt{?}\delta)\leq\Xi(\tau)
for
some
\tau
. By the definition of subtyping it must be the case that

\tau=\texttt{?}\delta^{\prime}
for some pattern
\delta^{\prime}
.

Since
\Xi(\texttt{?}\delta)\leq\Xi(\texttt{?}\delta^{\prime})
it follows
that
\Xi(\delta)\sqsubseteq\Xi(\delta^{\prime})
.

Since
\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi

we have that

\Xi(\gamma\odot\alpha)\sqsubseteq\Xi(\delta)
.

Therefore by transitivity of subtyping we have that

\Xi(\gamma\odot\alpha)\sqsubseteq\Xi(\delta^{\prime})
and
thus know that
\Xi
is a usable solution of

\{(\gamma\odot\alpha)\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta^{\prime}\}
.

Recomposing:

\frac{\alpha{\text{ fresh}}}{\texttt{!}\gamma\fatsemi\texttt{?}\delta^{\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\texttt{?}\alpha;\{(\gamma\odot\alpha)\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta^{\prime}\}}

Case
\varsigma_{1}=\texttt{?}\gamma
and
\varsigma_{2}=\texttt{!}\delta

Similar to the previous case.

∎

The desired result falls out as a corollary:

Corollary D.58 (Algorithmic environment join respects subtyping).

If:

•

\Theta_{1}\fatsemi\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi

•

\Xi
is some usable solution of
\Phi
such that

\Xi(\Theta_{1})\leq\Xi(\Theta_{3})
for some
\Theta_{3}

then

\Theta_{1}\fatsemi\Theta_{3}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\Phi^{\prime}
for some
\Theta^{\prime}
,

\Phi^{\prime}
such that
\Xi(\Theta)\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
and
\Xi
is
a usable solution of
\Xi(\Phi^{\prime})
.

Finally we want to see that algorithmic environment combination respects
subtyping.

Lemma D.59 (Algorithmic combination respects
subtyping).

If:

•

\Theta_{1}+\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi

•

\Xi
is some usable solution of
\Phi
such that

\Xi(\Theta_{1})\leq\Xi(\Theta_{3})
for some
\Theta_{3}

then

\Theta_{1}+\Theta_{3}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\Phi^{\prime}
for some
\Theta^{\prime}
,

\Phi^{\prime}
such that
\Xi(\Theta)\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
and
\Xi
is
a usable solution of
\Xi(\Phi^{\prime})
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

\Theta_{1}+\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi
.
∎

Corollary D.60 (Algorithmic combination respects subtyping (nullable
environments)).

If:

•

\Psi_{1}+\Psi_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi;\Phi

•

\Xi
is some usable solution of
\Phi
such that

\Xi(\Psi_{1})\leq\Xi(\Psi_{3})
for some
\Psi_{3}

then

\Psi_{1}+\Psi_{3}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi^{\prime};\Phi^{\prime}
for some
\Psi^{\prime}
,

\Phi^{\prime}
such that
\Xi(\Psi)\leq\Xi(\Psi^{\prime})
and
\Xi
is
a usable solution of
\Xi(\Phi^{\prime})
.

Relying on the previous results, we can now show the supertype checkability
lemma.

Lemma D.61 (Supertype checkability).

Suppose
\mathcal{P}
is closed.

•

If:

–

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi

–

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi

–

A\leq B

then
M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime}
,
where

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi^{\prime}
and

\Xi(\Theta)\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
.

•

If:

–

\{E\}\;\overrightarrow{G}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi;\,F

–

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi

–

A\leq B

then
\{E\}\;\overrightarrow{G}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\,B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime};\,F
where

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi^{\prime}
and
\Xi(\Theta)\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
.

•

If:

–

\{E\}\;G\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi;\,F

–

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi

–

A\leq B

then
\{E\}\;G\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\,B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime};\,F
where

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi^{\prime}
and
\Xi(\Theta)\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
.

Proof.

By mutual induction on the three premises. We concentrate on proving premise
1 in detail, and
TCG-Recv
for premise 3; premise 2 follows from
premise 3, and the remaining guard cases are straightforward.

By induction on the derivation of
M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi
.

Case
TC-Var

Assumption:

\frac{}{x\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,x:A;\,\emptyset}

Now given that we have
A\leq B
, we can construct:

\frac{}{x\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,x:B;\,\emptyset}

As
\Phi=\cdot
it straightforwardly follows that
\Xi
is a
usable solution, and since
A\leq B
we have that
x:A\leq x:B
as required.

Case
TC-Let

Assumption:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\lfloor T\rfloor\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{1};\,\Phi_{1}\quad N\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{2};\,\Phi_{2}\quad\quad\textsf{check}(\Theta_{2},x,\lfloor T\rfloor)=\Phi_{3}\quad\Theta_{1}\fatsemi\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi_{4}}{\textbf{{let}}\>x{{:T}}=M\>\textbf{{in}}\>N\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Phi_{4}}

By the IH we have that:

•

N\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{3};\,\Phi_{5}
for some
\Theta_{3}
,

\Phi_{5}

•

\Xi(\Theta_{2})\leq\Xi(\Theta_{3})

•

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Theta_{3}

By Corollary
D.58
we have that

\Theta_{1}\fatsemi\Theta_{3}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\Phi_{6}
, where
\Xi(\Theta)\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
and
\Xi
is a usable solution of

\Phi_{6}
.

By Lemma
D.51
, we have that

\textsf{check}(\Theta_{3},x,\lfloor T\rfloor)=\Phi_{5}

where
\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi_{5}
.

Therefore we can show that:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\lfloor T\rfloor\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{1};\,\Phi_{1}\quad N\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{3};\,\Phi_{4}\quad\quad\textsf{check}(\Theta_{3},x,\lfloor T\rfloor)=\Phi_{5}\quad\Theta_{1}\fatsemi\Theta_{3}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\Phi_{6}}{\textbf{{let}}\>x{{:T}}=M\>\textbf{{in}}\>N\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\,\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{4}\cup\Phi_{5}\cup\Phi_{6}}

as required.

Case
TC-Guard

\frac{\{E\}\;\overrightarrow{G}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi;\,\Phi_{1};\,F\quad\quad V\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\texttt{?}F^{{\bullet}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\,\Phi_{2}\quad\Psi+\Theta^{\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi_{3}}{\textbf{{guard}}\,V\,{:}\,E\,\{\overrightarrow{G}\}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}\cup\Phi_{3}}

By the IH:

•

\{E\}\;\overrightarrow{G}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi^{\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime}_{1};\,F

with
\Xi
a usable solution of
\Phi^{\prime}_{1}
and

\Xi(E)\sqsubseteq\Xi(F)

and
\Xi(\Psi)\leq\Xi(\Psi^{\prime})

•

V\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\texttt{?}F^{{\bullet}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime}_{2}

with
\Xi
a usable solution of
\Phi^{\prime}_{2}
and

\Xi(\Theta)\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime\prime})

By Corollary
D.60

\Psi^{\prime}+\Theta^{\prime\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime};\Phi^{\prime}_{3}
.

Recomposing:

\frac{\{E\}\;\overrightarrow{G}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi^{\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime}_{1};\,F\quad\quad V\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\texttt{?}F^{{\bullet}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime}_{2}\quad\Psi^{\prime}+\Theta^{\prime\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime};\Phi^{\prime}_{3}}{\textbf{{guard}}\,V\,{:}\,E\,\{\overrightarrow{G}\}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\cup\Phi^{\prime}_{2}\cup\Phi^{\prime}_{3}}

with
\Xi
a usable solution of
\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\cup\Phi^{\prime}_{2}\cup\Phi^{\prime}_{3}
and
\Xi(\Theta)\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})

as required.

Case
TC-Sub

Assumptions:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\quad A\leq A^{\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi_{2}}{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A^{\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}}

and:

•

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}

•

A^{\prime}\leq B

Since
A
,
A^{\prime}
, and
B
contain no pattern variables, by
Lemma
D.55
we have that

\textsf{pv}(\Phi_{2})=\emptyset
(however, since
\Xi
is a usable
solution of
\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}
, it follows that
\Phi_{2}

is satisfiable).

By Lemma
D.56
, we have that

A\leq B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi_{3}
, where
\Phi_{3}
is satisfiable.

Since
\Phi_{3}
is satisfiable and (again by
Lemma
D.55
)
\textsf{pv}(\Phi_{3})=\emptyset
, it follows that
\Xi
is a usable solution of

\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{3}
.

Thus by
TC-Sub
we have that:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\quad A\leq B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi_{3}}{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B^{\prime}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{3}}

where
\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{3}
,
as required.

Case
TCG-Recv

Assumption:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime},y:\texttt{?}\gamma^{{\bullet}};\,\Phi_{1}\quad\mathcal{P}({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})={\overrightarrow{\pi}}\quad\Theta=\Theta^{\prime}\mathop{-}\overrightarrow{x}\quad\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{\pi})\vee\textsf{base}(\Theta)\quad\textsf{check}(\Theta^{\prime},\overrightarrow{x},\overrightarrow{\lceil\pi\rceil})=\Phi_{2}}{\{E\}\;\textbf{{receive}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\texttt{[}\overrightarrow{x}\texttt{]}\;\textbf{{from}}\;y\mapsto M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}\cup\{E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma\};\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})}

Also we have that:

•

\Xi
is a usable solution of

\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}\cup\{E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma\}

•

\Xi(\tau)\leq\Xi(\sigma)

By the IH we have that

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime\prime},y:\delta^{{\bullet}};\,\Phi^{\prime}_{1}

where
\Xi(\Theta^{\prime},y:\texttt{?}\gamma^{{\bullet}})\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime\prime},y:\texttt{?}\delta^{{\bullet}})
and
where
\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi^{\prime}_{1}
.

By the definition of strict environment subtyping we have that

\texttt{?}\gamma\leq\texttt{?}\delta
and therefore
\gamma\sqsubseteq\delta
.

Let
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Theta^{\prime\prime}\mathop{-}\overrightarrow{x}
. It follows by the
definition of environment subtyping that
\Xi(\Theta)\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})
.

Due to the definition of the subtyping relation it remains the case that

\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{T})\vee\textsf{base}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})
.

By Lemma
D.51
we have that

\textsf{check}(\Theta^{\prime\prime},\overrightarrow{x},\lceil\overrightarrow{T}\rceil)=\Phi^{\prime}_{2}

where
\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi^{\prime}_{2}
.

Recomposing:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\tau\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime\prime},y:\texttt{?}\delta^{{\bullet}};\,\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\quad\mathcal{P}({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}}})={\overrightarrow{T}}\quad\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Theta^{\prime\prime}\mathop{-}\overrightarrow{x}\quad\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{T})\vee\textsf{base}(\Theta)\quad\textsf{check}(\Theta^{\prime},\overrightarrow{x},\overrightarrow{\lceil T\rceil})=\Phi^{\prime}_{2}}{\{E\}\;\textbf{{receive}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\texttt{[}\overrightarrow{x}\texttt{]}\;\textbf{{from}}\;y\mapsto M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\cup\Phi^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta\};\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot\delta}

where
\Xi({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot\gamma)\sqsubseteq\Xi({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot\delta)
and
\Xi
is a usable solution of

{\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\cup\Phi^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\delta\}}

and
\Xi(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta)
, as required.

∎

The following specific result, used within the completeness result, is a
corollary.

Corollary D.62 (Supertype checkability).

If:

•

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi

•

\mathcal{P}
is closed

•

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi

•

A\leq B

then there exist
\Theta^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime}
such that

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime}

where
\Xi
is a usable
solution of
\Phi^{\prime}
and

\Xi^{\prime}(\Theta)\preccurlyeq\Xi^{\prime}(\Theta^{\prime})
.

D.4.4.
Freshness of type variables

It is convenient to reason about fresh variables.

Definition D.63 (Created fresh).

A pattern variable
\alpha
is
created fresh
in a derivation

\mathbf{D}
if there exists some subderivation
\mathbf{D}^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{D}

which is of the form:

\frac{\alpha\text{ fresh}}{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}

Lemma D.64 (Pattern variable freshness).

If
\mathbf{D}=M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi
or

\mathbf{D}=M\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}_{\mathcal{P}}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi
where
\mathcal{P}

is closed, then all pattern
variables in
\textsf{pv}(\Theta)\cup\textsf{pv}(\Phi)
are created fresh in

\mathbf{D}
.

Proof.

By induction on the respective derivation, noting that since the signature
and types are closed, pattern variables are only introduced through the type
join and type merge operators, where they are created fresh.
∎

D.4.5.
Completeness proof

Finally, we can tie the above results together to show algorithmic completeness.

See
4.5

Proof.

A direct consequence of Lemma
D.65
.
∎

Lemma D.65 (Algorithmic Completeness (Generalised)).

•

If
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathcal{P}}M\,{:}\,A
where
\mathcal{P}
is closed, then
there exist some
\Theta,\Phi

and usable solution
\Xi
of
\Phi
such that

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi
where

\textsf{dom}(\Xi)=\textsf{pv}(\Theta)\cup\textsf{pv}(\Phi)
and

\Gamma\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta)
.

•

If
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathcal{P}}\overrightarrow{G}\,{:}\,A\;{::}\;F
where

E\vDash_{\textsf{lit}}F
for some pattern
E
and
\mathcal{P}
is closed, then
there exist some
\Theta,\Phi
and usable
solution
\Xi
of
\Phi
such that

\{E\}\;\overrightarrow{G}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi;\,F

where
\textsf{dom}(\Xi)=\textsf{pv}(\Theta)\cup\textsf{pv}(\Phi)

and

\Gamma\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta)
.

•

If
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathcal{P}}G\,{:}\,A\;{::}\;F
where

E\vDash_{\textsf{lit}}F
for some pattern
E
and

\mathcal{P}
is closed, then
there exist some
\Theta,\Phi
and usable
solution
\Xi
of
\Phi
such that

\{E\}\;G\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi;\,F

where
\textsf{dom}(\Xi)=\textsf{pv}(\Theta)\cup\textsf{pv}(\Phi)

and

\Gamma\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta)
.

Proof.

By mutual induction on both premises.

Premise 1:

Case
T-Var

Assumption:

\frac{}{x:A\vdash x\,{:}\,A}

Recomposing via
TC-Var
:

\frac{}{x\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,x:A;\,\emptyset}

with
\Xi=\cdot
.

Case
T-Const

Assumption:

\frac{c\text{ has base type }C}{\cdot\vdash c\,{:}\,C}

By
TS-Const
:

\frac{c\text{ has base type }C}{c\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;C\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\cdot;\,\emptyset}

By Lemma
D.42
we have that
c\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;C\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\cdot;\,\emptyset

with
\Xi=\cdot
, as required.

Case
T-App

Assumption:

\frac{\mathcal{P}(f)={\overrightarrow{A}\xrightarrow{}B}\quad(\Gamma_{i}\vdash V_{i}\,{:}\,A_{i})_{i\in 1..n}}{\Gamma_{1}+\cdots+\Gamma_{n}\vdash f(\overrightarrow{V})\,{:}\,B}

By (repeated) use of Lemma
D.41
, we have that there
exist some
\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\Gamma_{i}\leq\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}

and
V_{i}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A_{i}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Gamma^{\prime}_{i};\,\emptyset
for
i\in 1..n
.

By repeated use of Lemma
D.45
, we have that there

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots+\Gamma^{\prime}_{n}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi

for some
\Theta,\Phi
and that there
exists some usable solution
\Xi
of

\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
.

Thus by
TS-App
we can show

\frac{\mathcal{P}(f)={\overrightarrow{A}\xrightarrow{}B}\quad(V_{i}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A_{i}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Gamma^{\prime}_{i};\,\emptyset)_{i\in 1..n}\quad\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots+\Gamma^{\prime}_{n}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi}{f(\overrightarrow{V})\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi}

and by Lemma
D.42
we have that

f(\overrightarrow{V})\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi

as required.

Case
T-Let

Assumption:

\frac{\Gamma_{1}\vdash M\,{:}\,\lfloor T\rfloor\quad\Gamma_{2},x:\lfloor T\rfloor\vdash N\,{:}\,B}{\Gamma_{1}\triangleright\Gamma_{2}\vdash\textbf{{let}}\>x{{:T}}=M\>\textbf{{in}}\>N\,{:}\,B}

By the IH we have that:

•

There exist some
\Theta_{1},\Phi_{1}
and usable solution
\Xi_{1}
of
\Phi_{1}
such that

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{1};\,\Phi_{1}
where
\Gamma_{1}\preccurlyeq\Xi_{1}(\Theta_{1})

•

There exist some
\Theta_{2},\Phi_{2}
and usable solution
\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi_{2}
such that

N\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{2},x:\lfloor T^{\prime}\rfloor;\,\Phi_{2}
where
\Gamma_{2},x:\lfloor T\rfloor\preccurlyeq\Xi_{2}(\Theta_{2})

By Lemma
D.44
, we have that

\Theta_{1}\fatsemi\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi_{3}
and a usable
solution
\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
of
\Phi_{3}
such that

\Gamma_{1}\triangleright\Gamma_{2}\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta)
.

By Lemma
D.51
, we have that

\textsf{check}(\Theta_{2},x,\lfloor T\rfloor)=\Phi_{4}
and
\Xi

is a usable solution of
\Phi_{4}
.

Since
\Xi\supseteq\Xi_{1}\cup\Xi_{2}
and pattern variables in these
subderivations are only introduced fresh (Lemma
D.64
),
we have that
\Xi
is also a usable solution of
\Phi_{1}
and

\Phi_{2}
.

Therefore, we have that
\Xi
is a
usable solution of
\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}\cup\Phi_{3}\cup\Phi_{4}
.

Recomposing using
TC-Let
:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\lfloor T\rfloor\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{1};\,\Phi_{1}\quad N\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{2};\,\Phi_{2}\quad\quad\textsf{check}(\Theta_{2},x,\lfloor T\rfloor)=\Phi_{4}\quad\Theta_{1}\fatsemi\Theta_{2}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi_{3}}{\textbf{{let}}\>x{{:T}}=M\>\textbf{{in}}\>N\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Phi_{4}}

where
\Xi(\Theta)\preccurlyeq\Gamma_{1}\triangleright\Gamma_{2}

and

\Xi
is a usable solution of

\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}\cup\Phi_{3}\cup\Phi_{4}
, as required.

Case
T-Spawn

\frac{\Gamma\vdash M\,{:}\,\mathbf{1}}{\lceil\Gamma\rceil\vdash\textbf{{spawn}}\;M\,{:}\,\mathbf{1}}

By the IH

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\mathbf{1}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi
for some
\Theta,\Phi
,
and a usable solution
\Xi
such that

\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Phi)
.

Thus by
TS-Spawn
:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\mathbf{1}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi}{\textbf{{spawn}}\;M\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;\mathbf{1}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\lceil\Theta\rceil;\,\Phi}

where
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
and therefore
\lceil\Gamma\rceil\leq\lceil\Xi(\Theta)\rceil
.

Finally, by Lemma
D.42
, we have that

\textbf{{spawn}}\;M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\mathbf{1}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\lceil\Theta\rceil;\,\Phi
with usable
solution
\Xi
of
\Phi
as required.

Case
T-New

Assumption:

\frac{}{\cdot\vdash\textbf{{new}}\,{:}\,\texttt{?}\mathbb{1}^{{\bullet}}}

By
TS-New
we have that

\textbf{{new}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;\texttt{?}\mathbb{1}^{{\bullet}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\cdot;\,\emptyset

and by Lemma
D.42
it follows that

\textbf{{new}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\texttt{?}\mathbb{1}^{{\bullet}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\cdot;\,\emptyset
; we can set
solution
\Xi=\cdot
, as required.

Case
T-Send

Assumption:

\frac{\mathcal{P}({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})=\overrightarrow{T}\quad\Gamma_{\textit{target}}\vdash V\,{:}\,\texttt{!}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}^{\circ}\quad(\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\vdash W_{i}\,{:}\,\lceil T_{i}\rceil)_{i\in 1..n}}{\Gamma_{\textit{target}}+\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots+\Gamma^{\prime}_{n}\vdash V\,{!}\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\texttt{[}\overrightarrow{W}\texttt{]}\,{:}\,\mathbf{1}}

By the IH we have that:

•

V\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\texttt{!}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}^{\circ}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{\textit{target}};\,\Phi_{\textit{target}}

for some
\Theta_{\textit{target}}
,
\Phi_{\textit{target}}
and some usable solution
\Xi_{\textit{target}}
of
\Phi_{\textit{target}}

such that
\Gamma_{\textit{target}}\leq\Xi(\Theta_{\textit{target}})
.

•

(W_{i}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\lceil T_{i}\rceil\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{i};\,\Phi_{i})_{i\in 1..n}

for
\Theta_{i}
,
\Phi_{i}
and usable solutions
\Xi_{i}
of
\Phi_{i}
such that

\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\leq\Xi_{i}(\Theta_{i})

By repeated use of Lemma
D.45
we have that

\Theta_{\textit{target}}+\Theta_{1}+\ldots+\Theta_{n}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi_{\textit{env}}
, with some usable solution
\Xi_{\textit{env}}
of
\Phi_{\textit{env}}
such that
\Gamma+\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots+\Gamma^{\prime}_{n}\leq\Xi_{\textit{env}}(\Theta)
.

Since pattern variables are always chosen fresh (Lemma
D.64
) we have that

\Xi_{\textit{target}}\cup\Xi_{\textit{env}}\cup\bigcup_{i\in 1..n}\Xi^{\prime}_{i}
is a solution of
\Phi_{\textit{target}}\cup\Phi_{\textit{env}}\cup\bigcup_{i\in 1..n}\Phi^{\prime}_{i}
.

Thus we can show by
TS-Send
and Lemma
D.42
:

\frac{\frac{\mathcal{P}({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})=\overrightarrow{T}\hskip 8.19447ptV\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\texttt{!}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}^{\circ}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{\textit{target}};\,\Phi_{\textit{target}}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt(W_{i}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\lceil T_{i}\rceil\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta_{i};\,\Phi_{i})_{i\in 1..n}\hskip 8.19447pt\Theta_{\textit{target}}+\Theta_{1}+\ldots+\Theta_{n}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi_{\textit{env}}\hskip 8.19447pt}{V\,{!}\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\texttt{[}\overrightarrow{W}\texttt{]}\;{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0}\Rightarrow}\;\mathbf{1}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{\textit{target}}\cup\Phi_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Phi_{n}\cup\Phi_{\textit{env}}}}{V\,{!}\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\texttt{[}\overrightarrow{W}\texttt{]}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\mathbf{1}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{\textit{target}}\cup\Phi_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Phi_{n}\cup\Phi_{\textit{env}}}

where
\Xi
is a usable solution of

\Phi_{\textit{target}}\cup\Phi_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Phi_{n}\cup\Phi_{\textit{env}}

and
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
, as required.

Case
T-Guard

Assumption:

\frac{\Gamma_{1}\vdash V\,{:}\,\texttt{?}F^{{\bullet}}\quad\Gamma_{2}\vdash\overrightarrow{G}\,{:}\,A\;{::}\;F\quad E\sqsubseteq F\quad\vDash F}{{\Gamma_{1}}+{\Gamma_{2}}\vdash\textbf{{guard}}\,V\,{:}\,E\,\{\overrightarrow{G}\}\,{:}\,A}

By Lemma
D.41
we have that

V\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\texttt{?}F^{{\bullet}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Gamma^{\prime}_{1};\,\emptyset
where

\Gamma_{1}\leq\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}
.

By the IH we have that

\{E\}\;\overrightarrow{G}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi;\,F

where
\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi
and

\Gamma_{2}\leq\Psi
.

By Corollary
D.46
we have that

\Theta^{\prime}+\Psi\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi_{2}

with
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\leq\Xi(\Theta)
and where
\Xi
is a
solution of
\Phi_{2}
.

Recomposing:

\frac{\{E\}\;\overrightarrow{G}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi;\,\emptyset;\,F\quad\quad V\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;\texttt{?}F\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\,\emptyset\quad\Theta^{\prime}+\Psi\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\Phi}{\textbf{{guard}}\,V\,{:}\,E\,\{\overrightarrow{G}\}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi\cup\{E\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}F\}}

where
\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}
and

E\sqsubseteq\Xi(\gamma)
, as required.

Case
T-Sub

\frac{\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}\quad A\leq B\quad\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M\,{:}\,A}{\Gamma\vdash M\,{:}\,B}

By the IH, we have that there exist
\Theta,\Phi
and some usable solution
\Xi
of
\Phi
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Xi(\Theta)
and
M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi
.

By Lemma
D.62
we have that

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime}
where
\Xi
is a usable
solution of
\Phi^{\prime}
and
\Xi(\Theta)\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
.

Recalling that
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Xi(\Theta)
,
and noting that
\Xi(\Theta)\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
and
that
\Xi(\Theta)\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
, by the transitivity of subtyping
we have that
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
.

Therefore we have that:

•

M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime};\,\Phi^{\prime}

•

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi^{\prime}

•

\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})

as required.

Premise 2:

Case
TG-GuardSeq

\frac{(\Gamma\vdash G_{i}\,{:}\,A\;{::}\;E_{i})_{i\in I}}{\Gamma\vdash\overrightarrow{G}\,{:}\,A\;{::}\;E_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus E_{n}}

By repeated use of the IH (2) we have that

\{E_{i}\}\;G_{i}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi_{i};\,\Phi_{i};\,\gamma_{i}

for some
\Psi_{i},\Xi_{i},\gamma_{i}
such that

\Gamma\leq\Xi_{i}(\Psi_{i})
and
E\sqsubseteq\Xi_{i}(\gamma_{i})
for each
i\in I
.

By Corollary
D.49
we have that

\Psi_{1}\sqcap\ldots\sqcap\Psi_{n}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi_{\textit{env}};\Phi_{\textit{env}}

and some solution
\Xi_{\textit{env}}
of
\Phi_{\textit{env}}
such that

\Gamma\leq(\Xi_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Xi_{n}\cup\Xi_{\textit{env}})(\Psi_{\textit{env}})
.

Recomposing by
TCG-Guards
:

\frac{(\{E_{i}\}\;G_{i}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi_{i};\,\Phi_{i};\,\gamma_{i})_{i\in 1..n}\quad\gamma=\gamma_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\gamma_{n}\quad\Psi_{1}\sqcap\ldots\sqcap\Psi_{n}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Psi_{\textit{env}};\Phi_{\textit{env}}}{\{E\}\;\overrightarrow{G}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,\Psi_{\textit{env}}\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Phi_{\textit{env}}\cup\Phi_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Phi_{n};\,\gamma;\,}

Since pattern variables are generated fresh, we have that the pattern variables for each
\Xi_{i}
are disjoint.
Therefore, we have that:

•

\Xi=\bigcup_{i\in 1..n}\Xi_{i}\cup\Xi_{\textit{env}}
is a usable solution of
\Phi=\bigcup_{i\in 1..n}\Phi_{n}\cup\Phi_{\textit{env}}

•

\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Psi_{\textit{env}})

•

E\sqsubseteq\Xi(\gamma_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\gamma_{n})

as required.

Premise 3:

Case
TG-Fail

Assumption:

\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash\textbf{{fail}}\,{:}\,A\;{::}\;\mathbb{0}}

By
TCG-Fail
:

\frac{}{\{\mathbb{0}\}\;\textbf{{fail}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\top;\,\emptyset;\,\mathbb{0}}

Where
\mathbb{0}\sqsubseteq\mathbb{0}
and
\Gamma\leq\top

as required.

Case
TG-Free

\frac{\Gamma\vdash M\,{:}\,A}{\Gamma\vdash\textbf{{free}}\mapsto M\,{:}\,A\;{::}\;\mathbb{1}}

By the IH (1) we have that there exist
\Theta,\Phi
and usable solution

\Xi
of
\Phi
such that
M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi

with
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
.

Recomposing by
TCG-Free
:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi}{\{\mathbb{1}\}\;\textbf{{free}}\mapsto M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi;\,\mathbb{1}}

with
\Gamma\leq\Xi(\Theta)
and
\mathbb{1}\sqsubseteq\mathbb{1}
as
required.

Case
TG-Recv

Assumption:

\frac{\mathcal{P}({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})=\overrightarrow{T}\quad\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{T})\vee\textsf{base}(\Gamma)\quad\Gamma,y:\texttt{?}F^{{\bullet}},\overrightarrow{x}:\overrightarrow{\lceil T\rceil}\vdash M\,{:}\,B}{\Gamma\vdash\textbf{{receive}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\texttt{[}\overrightarrow{x}\texttt{]}\;\textbf{{from}}\;y\mapsto M\,{:}\,B\;{::}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot F}

We also assume that there is some
E
such that

E\vDash_{\textsf{lit}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot F
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma,y:\texttt{?}F^{{\bullet}},\overrightarrow{x}:\overrightarrow{\lceil T\rceil}
.

By the IH we have that there exist
\Theta,\Phi
and usable solution

\Xi
of
\Phi
s.t.
M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;A\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta,y:\texttt{?}\gamma^{{\bullet}};\,\Phi
where

\textsf{dom}(\Xi)=\textsf{pv}(\Theta)\cup\textsf{pv}(\Phi)
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta,y:\texttt{?}\gamma^{{\bullet}})
.

We next need to show that if
\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{T})\vee\textsf{base}(\Gamma)
implies that
\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{T})\vee\textsf{base}(\Theta)
. It suffices to show that
\textsf{base}(\Gamma)
implies

\textsf{base}(\Theta)
. Since
\Gamma\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta)
, by the definition of strict environment subtyping
it follows that if
\textsf{base}(\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta)
, then
\Gamma=\Theta
.

Next, since
\Gamma,y:\texttt{?}F^{{\bullet}},\overrightarrow{x}:\lceil\overrightarrow{T}\rceil\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime}),y:\texttt{?}\gamma^{{\bullet}}

it follows that
\Gamma,\overrightarrow{x}:\lceil\overrightarrow{T}\rceil\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta^{\prime})
and thus by
Corollary
D.52
we have that

\textsf{check}(\Theta,\overrightarrow{x},\overrightarrow{\lceil T\rceil})=\Phi_{2}
where

\Xi
is a usable solution of
\Phi_{2}
.

Next, since
\Gamma,y:\texttt{?}F^{{\bullet}}\preccurlyeq\Xi(\Theta),y:\texttt{?}\gamma^{{\bullet}}
it follows by the definition of
subtyping that
F\sqsubseteq\Xi(\gamma)
.

We have one final proof obligation: showing that
\Xi
solves

(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma
.

Since
E\vDash_{\textsf{lit}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot F
we have that
F\simeq E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}
and therefore both
F\sqsubseteq(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})

and
(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})\sqsubseteq F
.

Since
\texttt{?}F\leq\texttt{?}\Xi(\gamma)
we have that
F\sqsubseteq\Xi(\gamma)
. Thus by transitivity we have that
E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\sqsubseteq F\sqsubseteq\Xi(\gamma)
and therefore that

\Xi
solves

(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma
as necessary.

Thus, recomposing, we have:

\frac{M\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\;B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta^{\prime},y:\texttt{?}\gamma^{{\bullet}};\,\Phi_{1}\quad\mathcal{P}({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}}})={\overrightarrow{T}}\quad\Theta=\Theta^{\prime}\mathop{-}\overrightarrow{x}\quad\textsf{base}(\overrightarrow{T})\vee\textsf{base}(\Theta)\quad\textsf{check}(\Theta^{\prime},\overrightarrow{x},\overrightarrow{\lceil T\rceil})=\Phi_{2}}{\{E\}\;\textbf{{receive}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\texttt{[}\overrightarrow{x}\texttt{]}\;\textbf{{from}}\;y\mapsto M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.65}\Leftarrow}\,B\,\mathop{\mbox{\smaller$\blacktriangleright$}}\,\Theta;\,\Phi_{1}\cup\Phi_{2}\cup\{E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\mathop{\mathtt{<:}}\gamma\};\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}}\odot(E\mathop{/}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathttbf{m}})}

as required.

∎
